Completed means the initial curated cross-reference pass: the source verse and every listed target passage were read in the pinned Saints-Translation English records, and each retained pair has a specific explanation. It does not mean every possible biblical connection has been exhausted, or independent scholarly approval.

Leviticus 1:1

  1. Exodus 29:42-43 Earlier tent disclosure and speech announcement

    The earlier instruction locates divine disclosure and speech at the Tent of Testimony's doors, providing the particular announced setting for the Lord's speaking from the tent here.

    Read matching passage

    Exodus 29:42 as a continual sacrifice throughout your generations, at the doors of the Tent of Testimony before the Lord, where I will be made known to you, to speak to you.

    Exodus 29:43 There I will appoint Myself for the sons of Israel, and I will be sanctified in My glory.

    Pinned source record

    Read matching passage

    Both offerings use fine-flour loaves prepared with oil and unleavened wafers spread with oil. The earlier grain law explicitly calls its loaves unleavened as well; this praise-offering verse explicitly applies that description to the wafers.

    Read matching passage

    Leviticus 2:4 And if he brings a gift as a sacrifice baked in an oven, a gift unto the Lord from fine flour, it shall be unleavened loaves mixed with oil, and unleavened wafers spread with oil.

    Both praise and vow-completion deliverance offerings include oil-prepared fine-flour loaves and unleavened wafers spread with oil. Numbers explicitly describes a basket of unleavened bread accompanying the ram, while this praise-offering verse explicitly calls the wafers unleavened.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 6:14 and he shall bring his gift unto the Lord: one unblemished year-old male lamb for whole burning, and one unblemished year-old ewe lamb for sin, and one unblemished ram for deliverance,

    Numbers 6:17 And he shall prepare the ram as a sacrifice of deliverance unto the Lord, with the basket of unleavened bread; and the priest shall prepare its sacrifice and its drink offering.

  2. Hebrews 5:1-3 Later explanation of priestly offerings

    Hebrews explains that a human high priest offers for his own sins as well as for the people; Moses here directs Aaron to prepare both his own offerings and the people's gifts.

    Read matching passage

    Hebrews 5:1 For every high priest taken from among people is appointed on behalf of people in matters concerning God, to offer both gifts and sacrifices for sins.

    Hebrews 5:2 He can deal gently with those who are ignorant and wandering, since he too is surrounded by weakness,

    Hebrews 5:3 and because of it he is obliged to make offerings for sins on his own behalf, just as on behalf of the people.

    Aaron must offer for himself and then the people; Hebrews explicitly contrasts that priestly sequence with Christ's once-for-all self-offering.

    Read matching passage

    Hebrews 7:26 For such a high priest was indeed fitting for us: holy, innocent, undefiled, separated from sinners, and having become higher than the heavens.

    Hebrews 7:27 He has no daily need, as the high priests do, to offer sacrifices first for His own sins and then for those of the people; for He did this once for all when He offered Himself.

    Hebrews 7:28 For the law appoints people subject to weakness as high priests, but the word of the oath, which came after the law, appoints a Son, perfected for the age.

    Read matching passage

    Mark 7:20 He said, “What comes out of a person is what defiles the person.

    The festival law likewise identifies the seventh month's tenth day, soul humbling and no work for the Day of Atonement, adding convocation and evening-to-evening observance details beside this native-and-resident statute.

    Read matching passage

    Leviticus 23:27 And on the tenth day of this seventh month is the Day of Atonement; it shall be a holy convocation unto you, and you shall humble your souls and bring a whole burnt offering unto the Lord.

    Leviticus 23:28 You shall do no work on that very day, for this is a Day of Atonement for you, to make atonement for you before the Lord your God.

    Leviticus 23:29 Every soul that shall not be humbled on that very day shall be destroyed from among his people.

    Leviticus 23:30 And every soul that does work on that very day, that soul shall perish from among his people.

    Leviticus 23:31 You shall do no work. This is an everlasting ordinance throughout your generations in all your dwellings.

    Leviticus 23:32 It shall be a Sabbath of Sabbaths unto you, and you shall humble your souls; from the ninth day of the month, from evening unto evening, you shall keep your Sabbath.

Leviticus 16:30

For on this day he shall make atonement for you, to cleanse you from all your sins before the Lord; and you shall be cleansed.

  1. Hebrews 9:13-14 Later comparison of sacrificial cleansing and conscience cleansing

    Hebrews compares animal blood sanctifying for fleshly cleanness with Christ's blood cleansing the conscience. This verse announces annual atonement and cleansing from sins before the Lord, retaining its own stated promise beside the later comparison.

    Read matching passage

    Hebrews 9:13 For if the blood of goats and bulls, and the ashes of a heifer sprinkling those who have been defiled, sanctify for the cleanness of the flesh,

    Hebrews 9:14 how much more will the blood of Christ, who through an eternal Spirit offered Himself unblemished to God, cleanse our conscience from dead works to serve the living God!

Leviticus 16:32

The priest whom they anoint and whose hands they consecrate to serve as priest after his father shall make atonement. He shall put on the linen robe, a holy robe,

Leviticus 16:33

and he shall make atonement for the Holy of Holies, and for the Tent of Testimony and the altar shall he make atonement; and for the priests and for the whole congregation shall he make atonement.

  1. Leviticus 16:16-20 Same-chapter concluding enumeration of the earlier atonement scope

    The earlier instructions name holy-place and tent atonement, Aaron and his house and congregation, then altar cleansing and cleansing for the priests. This conclusion gathers the holy places, altar, priests and congregation into its stated scope.

    Read matching passage

    Leviticus 16:16 and make atonement for the holy place from the sons of Israel’s uncleannesses and their wrongdoings, concerning all their sins. He shall do the same for the Tent of Testimony established among them amid their uncleanness.

    Leviticus 16:17 And no one shall be in the Tent of Testimony when he enters to make atonement in the holy place until he comes out. He shall make atonement for himself, his house, and the whole congregation of the sons of Israel.

    Leviticus 16:18 And he shall go out unto the altar opposite the Lord and make atonement upon it. He shall take some of the calf’s blood and some of the male young goat’s blood and put it all around on the altar’s horns,

    Leviticus 16:19 and he shall sprinkle some of the blood on it with his finger seven times, cleanse it, and consecrate it from the sons of Israel’s uncleannesses.

    Leviticus 16:20 And he shall finish making atonement for the holy place, the Tent of Testimony, and the altar, and perform cleansing for the priests. He shall present the living male young goat.

Leviticus 16:34

And this shall be an everlasting statute unto you, to make atonement for the sons of Israel from all their sins; it shall be done once in the year, as the Lord directed Moses.

  1. Exodus 30:10 Related annual atonement statute at the altar

    Exodus likewise commands once-a-year purification with sin-offering blood on the altar's horns. This conclusion gives the annual statute for atonement for Israel's sins, preserving the different stated objects and scope.

    Read matching passage

    Exodus 30:10 Once a year Aaron shall make expiation for it on its horns. With the blood of purification for sins, for expiation, once a year he shall purify it throughout their generations. It is most holy to the Lord.

    Pinned source record

  2. Hebrews 9:25-26 Later contrast between yearly entry and Christ's single sacrifice

    Hebrews expressly contrasts the high priest's year-by-year entry with another's blood with Christ's single appearance to put away sin through His sacrifice. This verse supplies the source rite's once-in-the-year conclusion.

    Read matching passage

    Hebrews 9:25 Nor did He enter to offer Himself many times, as the high priest enters the holy places year by year with another’s blood,

    Hebrews 9:26 for then He would have had to suffer many times since the foundation of the world. But now, once at the end of the ages, He has appeared to set aside sin through His sacrifice.
